The stairs creaked just as dramatically as Voldemort expected them to when Andromeda led them upstairs. Various dour family portraits along the stairwell inspected Harry and Voldemort with disdain, but kept silent about their opinions on blood purity. As per Harry’s accounting of the future, Walburga did not show such restraint when she herself was eventually painted into a portrait… likely by Taxomin Thomas, if it was that lifelike.

What could have convinced an aesthete like Thomas, whose standards only Harry’s incomparable soul could meet, to agree to paint a harpy like Walburga?

Copious amounts of money, most likely, and Wizengamot votes in favour of the legalisation of soul magic.

Once upstairs, Andromeda led them past a series of locked rooms to a blue-painted door with moving stars glistening upon it.

“The stars were Narcissa’s idea,” Voldemort overheard Andromeda whisper to Harry, as Walburga eyed them with displeasure. “Our mother deems them… frivolous.”

“Such fancies are unsuited to young men,” Walburga sniffed.

“Mother, they’re babies.” This was Bellatrix, whose sneer curled serpentine and venomous upon her ruby lips. “Far from men, yet.”

“But what sort of men will they become if this rampant silliness is encouraged? Effeminate, impotent milksops, surely, incapable of siring Pureblood heirs.”

Narcissa tilted her head and commented coolly, almost meditatively, “Perhaps reflecting on children’s potential sexual potency is an inappropriate topic for civilised company.” She gestured politely at Voldemort and Harry, then added, “And an inappropriate topic for civilised thought.”

Walburga let out an enraged wheeze, which, combined with her incensed magic, made it whistle like a tea-kettle. “Are you calling me uncivilised?”

Narcissa placed her hand upon her chest as though shocked, though her eyes remained cool as ever. “Never, Mother,” she deadpanned. From the surface of her mind, Voldemort read, I’d rather call you depraved.

Ha. Legilimency was proving to be a source of entertainment in the Black household. It dawned on Voldemort that Narcissa, an heiress doubtlessly trained in Occlumency since she could walk, must have let that thought float to the surface deliberately for him or Harry to pick up. It seemed the Black sisters were conspiring to reveal unsavoury information about their mother—Andromeda through whispers, Narcissa through an intentionally unguarded mind, and Bellatrix through sheer malice. If looks could kill, no Avada Kedavra would be required for Bellatrix to send her mother to the afterlife.

“How lovely!” Harry exclaimed inanely, as if he hadn’t heard a word that had been spoken. He must be hanging on to his sanity and his composure by main force. “Could we, maybe, I don’t know, open the door now?”

“Of course!” Walburga jolted forward to rotate the golden, moon-shaped handle—likely also Narcissa’s doing—and opened the door.

Instantly, noise filled the corridor—the babbling of a toddler and what sounded like Exploding Snap. Which meant that the room had been Silenced. There was a blue silk curtain behind the door, too, obscuring the room and dotted with yet more stars… a typical curtain of Concealment layered with extra charms to prevent anyone under ten from exiting it, as though the room were a jail and the children within prisoners, or, worse, exotic animals caged in a menagerie.

“Why,” asked Voldemort delicately, a thrill running through him at Harry’s darkening eyes, “is a children’s nursery Silenced and Concealed?”

Walburga tittered nervously. “Why, it’s… We adults have business to attend to, my lord, and our daughters have their studies. It’s convenient for all involved if the boys are not seen or heard, at their age. That is how children ought to be.”

A strange, coiling tension began to wind its way across Harry’s magic, and, when Voldemort checked, Harry’s shoulders. “Not seen or heard.” Harry pivoted to face Walburga, and Voldemort nearly gasped in delight at how predatory the snarl on Harry’s face was, how sharp-seeming his teeth. A dragon’s fangs. A deadly promise. “I’ve heard that before. From the woman who raised me, in fact.”

“Well!” Walburga preened, impervious to her own impending demise. “There you are, girls! See what happens when the heir of a noble house is raised properly? Our lord must be so very proud.”

“Ecstatic,” Voldemort drawled, caught between wanting to murder Harry’s Muggle relatives—again—and wanting to feel Harry’s snarl under his own fingers, to feel those soft lips, those sharp teeth. To be bitten by them. To be tasted.

Harry stepped through the curtain into the room, Andromeda still attached to him. Voldemort followed while the rest stayed outside, with Walburga poised on the threshold. There wasn’t enough space on the inside for all of them.

The playroom was scarcely large enough to be habitable, despite the expansion charms Voldemort detected; the fact that he could sense them meant that they were inexpertly cast, likely by a young witch or wizard… one of the sisters, mayhap, a few years ago, to give their newborn siblings more space to play. To live. To breathe.

Was imprisoning infants in a glorified storage closet part of Walburga’s disciplinary parenting? What benefit could it possibly have, even in her diseased mind? The reality Voldemort saw before him beggared belief. While the Black residence was not as vast as the Malfoys’, it had palatial rooms aplenty. Yet its two male heirs were jointly housed in a single playroom not big enough to contain more than four adults.

As though it was intended that the children never see any adults. As though the children were supposed to be abandoned in here. On their own.

And they were, aside from an aged, crooked, remarkably hideous house-elf, who tottered forward and bowed in subservience… all while strategically keeping the youngest brother, Regulus, behind him. The house-elf was old and beaten and scarred and missing parts of his drooping ears, the delicate cartilage torn through with holes that matched the diameter of a wand-tip, as if they had been stabbed by a wand. But even his diminutive frame was enough to shield the baby.

A baby that tilted sideways, leaning over to sit on one buttock, just so he could see past the house-elf and goggle at his new visitors with wide, wondering eyes. Drool stretched from his chubby chin to a colourful wooden rattle that he had evidently been chewing, given the tooth-shaped indentations upon it. He said, with such pure, hopeful confusion that it pierced even Voldemort’s heart, “Da… da?”

“He’s not Dad,” said the other child grimly, perhaps two years older than the first, with some of the baby fat having melted off him. He had a peculiarly jaded air for a three-year-old who had just been playing Exploding Snap with a house-elf. Or maybe it wasn’t so peculiar, given the child’s status as a prisoner under house arrest. Sirius—for it had to be Sirius—stood up on sturdy little legs clad in yellow, Hippogriff-printed pyjamas. His black hair was almost as wild around his face as Bellatrix’s, and he levelled Voldemort and Harry with a truly impressive glare, a glare that only wavered when he caught sight of his sister, Andromeda. “Who… who’re these people, Andy? Whaddoo they want?”

Before Andromeda could reply, Walburga had swept forward, pushing her daughter aside and looming over Sirius like the Grim Reaper; Sirius flinched back. “Her name is not Andy, Heir Black,” Walburga enunciated. “You shall address her properly or not at all.” She waved at Andromeda. “Leave. He needs to learn that if he does not address a lady properly, then he shall have none visit him. Ever.”

“B-ban all the sisters from visiting?” Andromeda demanded in disbelief. “But we’re the only human beings the boys meet! Mother—”

“Quiet!” Walburga barked. “Sirius must learn his manners. I shall not have our house’s good name tarnished in front of our lord by embarrassments who cannot even use the correct forms of address.”

Regulus whimpered. He may not have understood Walburga’s threat to deny him access to the only affectionate family he had, but he clearly felt it. Sirius did not cry, as if he was all cried out, but he did shrink in on himself and take a silent, protective step back towards his brother.

It had also not escaped Voldemort’s notice that when Sirius had flinched, the lad’s hand had automatically shot up to cradle his left shoulder, indicating the presence of an injury there, either as a result of spell-work or just simple, cruel wrenching. As the matron of Wool’s Orphanage had often wrenched Tom.

But while Sirius retreated, the house-elf quickly hobbled up to Walburga and threw himself down at her feet. “Mistress!” he wailed at a pitch so high, it all but perforated Voldemort’s eardrums. “Beloved Mistress, it is Kreacher’s fault! Kreacher should have taught the boys better, punish Kreacher, please!”

Voldemort raised an eyebrow. That was self-sacrificing even for a house-elf, to deflect Walburga Black’s unholy anger onto itself.

Kreacher was now clutching the hem of Walburga’s robes as a worshipper might the robes of a god, with his increasing abjection visibly appeasing and calming Walburga. It was vaguely sickening to watch, especially since it only gave Walburga more control over her sons and more ways of threatening them—that she could harm Kreacher, who was obviously their primary carer, if they did not behave. Still, the house-elf persisted, for not to do so would be to let his small charges be harmed instead... which was even worse. “Punish Kreacher,” the house-elf begged, voice cracking with desperation. “Punish only Kreacher, it will be enough. Kreacher will iron his hands and dash his head against the wall and—”

“You will do no such thing, Kreacher,” Harry spoke at last, his command ringing with as much magical compulsion as an Imperio. It was stunning.

Harry had been ramrod-straight through it all, standing stock-still and silent, like a man reliving a nightmare… or becoming a nightmare.

Voldemort studied his son’s countenance and found that, yes, Harry’s eyes were bright with unshed tears, but bright with rage, too. Incandescent, even. He was trembling minutely all over, as if his magic was clawing at his insides to get out, as if he was on the verge of losing his physical limitations, his very body, to become a being of raw magic instead—wrath-driven and full of bloodlust, beautiful and terrifying as an Obscurus. The magical equivalent of a hurricane. Sweat beaded on Harry’s skin, dampening his temples as he struggled to hold all that magic in. There was an awful, ominous crackling of energy around his clenched fists, a localised lightning that singed Andromeda’s fingertips so badly that she snatched her hand away, stumbling backwards.

As for Voldemort, it was all he could do to not hurtle willingly into that electric fire, into that searing heat, and to have Harry reduce him to ashes. Yes, darling. Do it. Do it…

Walburga finally registered alarm. “Our lord’s heir, is everything all ri—”

“Quiet.” Harry echoed the word back at her, sonorous and deep and resounding with magic, but he also whipped out his hand towards her as he did so, and Walburga just…

…stopped.

Voldemort gaped. He was, at first, shocked that Harry had somehow found a means of killing someone wandlessly and wordlessly, a feat even Voldemort had never achieved. But then he realised that Walburga was very much alive, just… frozen. And not by an Impedimenta, either. While Walburga still registered as alive to Voldemort’s magic, she lacked a pulse or a heartbeat or any indicator of biological activity at all. She was not breathing; she was not blinking; she was not perspiring; she was not thinking. She was a complete blank. Even Voldemort’s Legilimency could not reach her.

“What—” Andromeda’s eyes were huge. Bellatrix grabbed her to pull Andromeda out of Harry’s warpath, but Bellatrix’s own starving eyes lingered upon Harry as if upon a feast, as if upon the first glimpse of freedom that she had ever had.

“He stopped her Time,” Bellatrix hissed to Andromeda, euphoria making her hoarse. “Can’t you tell? He stopped a force no wizard or witch has ever been able to stop. Until now.”

Ah. Yes. Voldemort should’ve known it was temporal magic, akin to what had brought Harry here; Voldemort would’ve picked up on it as soon as his shock had worn off. Still, Bellatrix climbed several notches in his regard. Magical diagnostics were notorious for being difficult at best and impossible at worst, but Bellatrix had correctly identified a magic so rare that few could even recognise it. Let alone use it, as Harry had, likely as a consequence of already having travelled through Time.

Exceptional, irresistible Harry.

“I’m sorry,” Harry snuffled, wetly and unsteadily, peering down at Sirius and Regulus with horrified guilt, as if he were a werewolf and they unsuspecting lambs that had wandered into his lair. “That—that must’ve been really scary, right? I’ll fix her immedia—”

“Don’t,” said Sirius abruptly, and then flushed. He fidgeted and, glancing up at Harry from under his curly fringe, ventured coyly, “I… I think… It’s not hurting Mummy, right? She’s just resting? Can’t she stay resting for a while?” Sirius became more animated, more excited. “Maybe till my shoulder doesn’t go ouchie anymore and Reggie can pee again?”

“I…” Harry trailed off in confusion before continuing. “Wha… What do you mean by peeing again?”

Narcissa took her turn in yanking Bellatrix out of the room so that she could enter, instead—a revolving door of sisters that would have been amusing were Voldemort not currently obsessed with cataloguing the temporal anomaly that was Walburga, and tracing every thread of magic leading from her back to Harry. Back to his extraordinary son, who could stop Time itself in its tracks. Who needed Horcruxes if what Harry had done could be controlled on a cellular level, to halt the ageing process and keep one alive forever?

Narcissa had no such philosophical questions, however. Her mind, still a purposely open book, was only filled with urgent concern for her brothers. She pinned Harry with a stern look, her usually light, silvery gaze iron-dark, and said, “It is a common tortur—discipline method of our mother’s. She magically prevents the boys—and us girls, too, when we were littler—from urinating, even if we physically have to, until we’re in unbearable agony and begging for it to stop. Then she’ll let us go, but if she thinks we misbehaved gravely enough, we get the special treatment of her releasing her hold on our bladders not in a toilet, but in front of the family and our house-elves, so that we can piss ourselves in public and be humiliated.” The word ‘piss’ in Narcissa’s refined tone was jarring, as was the horror that crept up on Voldemort at her description.

To subject children to that... magical children… And for what? What could such torment accomplish against an infant? A toddler? Voldemort regularly tortured adults, yes, and robbed them of their dignity often, but only if it had a calculable, measurable benefit. Going after children this young just seemed… perverse. Perverse, ineffective and unhinged.

Harry had gone still again. His magic buffeted about him like a storm-wind, picking up speed and rifling through his hair. Light magic flashed within him and lit his eyes a blazing lightning-white, only to abruptly settle, as though a great downward pressure had been exerted upon it—the pressure of Harry’s will, immense and unshakable. A will so oppressively stifling and heavy that it weighed down the very air itself, thickening it and momentarily rendering it hard to breathe. Then the wind died off and normalcy returned.

Harry fell to his knees before the boys and extended his arms towards them. “She’ll never do any of that to you again,” he said fiercely, his whole body shaking. “She’ll never—never—I won’t let her.”

Sirius hung back, cautious, but Regulus zeroed in on the sight of two arms open to him and toddled towards them at top speed, as though hugs were such a rare delicacy that they could not conceivably be resisted. Perhaps Walburga had yet to unleash direct corporal punishment on him, and physical touch was yet to become a source of fear for him.

“Y-young Master!” Kreacher reached out to keep Regulus from the embrace of this frighteningly powerful stranger, but Regulus was already hopping into Harry’s arms, curling up in them like a snakeling in its nest. He shot his elder brother a look so imploring that Sirius grudgingly joined them, too, half-in and half-out of the hug, a foot planted outside of Harry’s right knee in case he had to escape. When a surge of healing magic flowed from Harry into the boys, undoing Sirius’ injury and Walburga’s unnatural stranglehold on Regulus’ bladder, it briefly encased them in a soft radiance. Light magic at its most tender.

Voldemort heard Andromeda stifle a sob.

Awestruck, Sirius patted his healed shoulder testingly, and Regulus wriggled happily in his nappy.

“I’ve had that happen to me, too.” Harry ran his hand over Sirius’s curls. “Well, not in that particular way, but I’ve—I know. I know.” Harry’s voice broke. “And I’m so, so sorry that happened to you. It should never have happened to you. It’ll never happen again.”

“But I’m a bad kid,” Sirius muttered into Harry’s collar, almost angrily, like Harry was at fault for questioning Walburga’s parenting, even though Sirius himself hated it. “That’s what bad kids deserve.”

“You’re not a bad kid.” Harry pulled back a bit to meet Sirius’s eyes head-on. “You’re going to grow up to be s-such a wonderful godfa—such a wonderful man, such a loving—even if you’re put through hell, you’ll never let that break you, never let that take your ability to love away from you. You’ll be incredibly strong. And you,” Harry turned to Regulus, “You’re going to be so brave. You’re going to be a hero.”

Regulus’s eyes went very, very round. Sirius asked hesitantly, “A… hero? Like… like Lord Pimplerimple?”

Harry nodded, despite clearly having no idea what that name was. “Like Lord Pimplerimple.”

Regulus clapped and giggled, though his snort-like giggle bore a distinct resemblance to a bout of cheerful flatulence. The moment Regulus laughed, Sirius relaxed, as though getting a laugh out of his brother was a crucial test of trust, and Harry had just passed. Or perhaps Sirius had deduced that anyone willing to say “Pimplerimple” in complete seriousness couldn’t be that bad of a person.

Voldemort’s heart palpitated oddly. This was the godfather Harry so revered, the father figure who was Voldemort’s rival, and Harry was holding him tighter than he’d ever held Voldemort.

Voldemort should be feeling envy. He should be aboil with vengefulness. But the sight of Harry with children in his arms, smiling into their hair, had—had an impact on Voldemort that he could not explain. He brimmed with an uncomfortable warmth, a warmth that was alien to him, for though Harry had introduced him to passions that burned flame-hot, this mellow, disconcertingly invasive warmth was new.

Voldemort envisioned Harry back in their study, reclining on their couch, reading to a child with Tom Riddle’s well-groomed hair and aloof expression, a child whose aloofness melted away as he drifted off to sleep to the cadence of Harry’s storytelling, to the rhythm of Harry’s heartbeat. Voldemort envisioned himself there, watching over them or perhaps curled around them, and he—

He—

“Kreacher.”

Harry’s utterance of the house-elf’s name startled Voldemort out his uncharacteristic reverie. What was wrong with him? He had never wanted to share Harry. And he still didn’t. What madness was this?

“Kreacher,” Harry repeated, when the ancient house-elf cringed. “Thank you,” Harry said. “Thank you for protecting them with everything you have.”

Kreacher’s eyes, too, grew round. Harry seemed to have that effect on people, be they human or not.

“Please do keep protecting them. But I swear, I swear—” at the palpable weight of Harry’s magical oath, Kreacher’s eyeballs bulged in their sockets “—that you’ll never have to protect them from their mother again.”

“Kreacher adores his Mistress,” Kreacher mumbled miserably. “Kreacher raised Mistress from when she was born, just like he raised Master Sirius and Master Regulus.”

“It’s okay, Kreacher,” Harry said gently. Everything about him was so gentle now, as if his very soul were woven from gossamer, airy and insubstantial and luminescent. His magic was the diametric opposite of what it had just been, a soothing glow as opposed to a gathering storm. “I get it. It’s okay.”

Kreacher pled pitifully, “You… You won’t hurt her, Master Sir?”

Harry’s lips thinned, but he nodded. He rose carefully, depositing a pouting Sirius and a clingy Regulus in Kreacher’s waiting lap. “I’ll be back in a couple of minutes,” he told the boys kindly, his smile lovelier than a sunrise, but then he whirled towards Voldemort and grabbed his elbow. “I just need to talk with my dad, all right?”

Harry dragged Voldemort out of the room, past the gawking sisters, down the stairs, and into the lounge area they’d come from. Harry crowded Voldemort against a mahogany cabinet despite being far shorter, his green eyes intent.

Voldemort caught his breath. “Harry…”

“We have to save them.” Harry tore himself away to pace back and forth across the room, and Voldemort swallowed his regret at the distance between them. “We have to, I’m not leaving this house until we can—can—” Harry gesticulated incomprehensibly “—kill Walburga without killing her?”

“Why not kill her?” Voldemort asked honestly.

Harry glowered at him. “Because I’m not going to kill these children’s mother, like… like my own was killed.”

“By me.”

Harry shut his eyes, pained, then reopened them, steady and resilient as ever. “By the other you. And you’re not him anymore. Are you?”

“I’m the fool with a functioning Patronus and an absolute lack of self-control when it comes to granting my son’s wishes,” Voldemort replied. “So, yes, I suppose. I’m not him anymore.”

“Exactly,” Harry said firmly. “I’m not making any more orphans. So no mother-killing, no matter how tempting it may be.”

Voldemort drew close to him and extended a finger to run along the rim of Harry’s collar, where it met his skin. Harry shivered. “But you were tempted, weren’t you? Very, very tempted. It was a sight to behold.”

Harry huffed. “Still. I’m not... I’ll never be a murderer. I don’t have that in me, although it feels like I do when I go stark raving mad.” He paused, plainly discomfited by his own violent impulses. “But I can’t leave Walburga frozen in Time, either. That’s just creepy. Even evil, maybe. Like mummifying a person while they’re still alive.” Harry stared at his hands, appalled. “I had no clue I could even do that.”

That was his Harry, indeed; conquering temporal magic with no training and no preparation, and being unnerved by his own power instead of exploiting it. “You need only give me an hour or two with her,” Voldemort cajoled. “I will journey so deep into her psyche that she won’t even realise I’m changing her from within. I’ll make it impossible for her mind to sustain any chain of thought about hurting, punishing or verbally abusing her children, and if she can’t think about it, she won’t be able to do it. She’ll be de-fanged.”

Harry groaned and buried his face in his hands. “Fuck, that sounds… twisted. Isn’t that the same as taking away her freedom of will? Messing about in her head and cutting any wires that don’t lead where you want them to?”

“Do you deem it safe for her sons—or her daughters, or even her husband—for her to retain said freedom of will?” Voldemort knew his suggestion was reasonable. It was the most logical alternative to murder, though it could be considered a spiritual murder, of sorts. A metaphysical amputation.

“Fuck,” said Harry again. “I guess... at least we’re not killing her or torturing her. This is the only non-violent way to… to get her to not be a vomit-inducing child abuser, without traumatising Sirius and Regulus even more by doing something unspeakable to their mum. Maybe she’ll even be a nicer mum to them if she can’t think about all the horrid things she usually thinks about. Maybe Sirius won’t grow up hating this house.” Harry scratched his head. “But will your, um, spell carry on working once we go back to my timeline?”

“The spell will be permanent,” Voldemort reassured him. “My dear, you need not worry. If you keep up your pretence of being seduced by her daughters, Walburga will gladly leave you alone with them. In the meanwhile, I’ll make intimations about the contents of the Black vault and the inventory of their library, as per our original plan, and she’ll think I’m attempting to ascertain what precious texts or artefacts we might ask for as a dowry. She’ll be delighted to accompany me to the manor’s library to begin our search, where I’ll gradually rearrange her mind while you stay with the sisters upstairs. Keep them entertained; keep them distracted. In two hours, I’ll be done, and I’ll come upstairs to collect you.”

“And Walburga will be changed forever.”

Voldemort smiled with all of his teeth. “She will never be the same.”

Harry looked ill. “Joy,” he murmured, flatly. “I’m agreeing to Dark mind magic that Hermione would probably disown me for. If friends can disown friends. Even though she used Memory Charms on her own—never mind.”

Hermione. Harry’s bibliophilic friend, for it was in the bookshop that Harry had initially mentioned her. “If she is as well-read and intellectual as you imply,” Voldemort said, “then your friend would comprehend the benefits of… editing Walburga, in these circumstances.”

“‘Editing’ an actual person, not just their memories? Uh, no, Hermione would just say that the children should be removed from the abusive home and that the abuser should face justice in a court of law.”

“Could any justice be sufficient punishment for what Walburga did to her own children?”

Harry looked away.

“Sweetheart.” Voldemort cupped Harry’s chin, lifting it until Harry met his eyes. “Only you and I understand—and your Sirius, perhaps, and the Black sisters. We have all been tortured as children. We know there is no court of law that could mete out a sufficient punishment for what we have endured. Those who have not been abused will never understand. They cannot.” He dragged his thumb slowly along Harry’s cheekbone, relishing how Harry’s eyelids fluttered. “Do not burden yourself with guilt over this. As you said, the alternatives would be even worse.”

“How much worse?” Harry rasped. “What if… What if I just removed the boys like Hermione would’ve suggested?”

“If Sirius and Regulus were truly removed from their home, suffocating though it is, it would be traumatising to them to lose the place where they have lived with their doting sisters and their, to my understanding, non-abusive father, at least when that father is not recuperating from his wife’s attempted murders. Is it not far better for them to simply have a safer home where they already are, instead of being split up from each other and adopted or fostered into different families, families that may have troubles of their own?” Voldemort tapped Harry’s nose. “You were fostered, Harry. How well did that go? Were you ever at home there? Were you ever treated as if you were part of the family?”

Harry frowned. “They were my relatives. And not all foster families are like that. I just… It was just my luck of the draw.”

“Harry.”

“I could adopt the boys myself. I could… We could…”

“We could, what, take them with us to another timeline when we leave? A timeline where there is an active war underway and where they will be even more unsafe? How will we protect them if we are simultaneously battling my future self and his brainless minions? And even if we do manage to protect the boys or hide them away, won’t being abducted by us in itself not be traumatising for them, being dragged away from their sisters, their father, their home, their world?”

“Bellatrix killed Sirius in that timeline.” Harry shuddered. “Under that Voldemort’s indirect command, but still. They hated each other by then. Imagine everything Walburga would’ve done to them to make them like that, to make Bellatrix like that. She seems so devoted to Sirius now.”

“Bellatrix is on the knife-edge of sanity. It would not take much for her to tumble off that edge.”

“Thanks for the reminder,” Harry said dryly. “And I’m supposed to flirt with her for the next two hours?”

“Do your best,” Voldemort said. “Without letting her touch you, of course.”

“Wow. Did you just italicise an entire sentence? I could hear the italics. Amazing.”

“Harry,” Voldemort growled. “You may flirt to keep your cover, but you are not to allow any of them to be forward with you.”

“No forwardness, despite me literally inviting forwardness. Got it.” Harry squared his shoulders. “Mission Pretend to Be Seduced: launch.”

Chapter 21


Chapter Summary

Voldemort exercises extraordinary self-control. Walburga gets shipped directly to hell with a "Do Not Return to Sender" stamp on the box. Narcissa just wants Bellatrix to maybe not get herself killed.



Chapter Notes

bellatrix wanting to fuck both harry and tom at the same time:



***



Finally! An update after so long! My apologies, beloved readers, but real life was shitty in that special way only real life can be, and I had to retire from fandom for a while. But I’m back! (Sort of. I’m back intermittently, is what I’m saying.)

This is, as of 26 November 2024, a heavily edited version of the chapter I originally posted, which contained a very dark (and triggering for many) interlude from the point of view of Bellatrix. Based on the feedback of readers who felt it was too dark for the overall light tone of this story, I have now removed that section and will publish it as an independent one-shot in the series of one-shots that I've been intending to write from other, minor characters' perspectives, to be published upon the main story's completion. Thank you so much to all the amazing reviewers who provided their thoughtful feedback and helped me make this a better story! You rock!

And a quick recap on the sisters’ ages, which I have changed to better fit the plot: Bellatrix is eighteen, Andromeda is sixteen and Narcissa is thirteen. I’ve changed the layout of 12 Grimmauld Place, too, just to accommodate the various shenanigans in this chapter.



See the end of the chapter for more notes



When Voldemort and Harry re-entered the nursery, they found all three Black sisters sitting cross-legged on the carpeted floor in a most unladylike manner, with Regulus waddling back and forth between them like an eager duckling, getting hugs from each of them. Sirius was solemnly plating conjured toy food onto thick wooden discs that were presumably from a stacking game.

Harry’s countenance softened at the sight. “Hello again!” he smiled as Regulus launched himself at Harry’s legs, wrapping his tiny, plump arms around them and coming up short. Nestling a gentle hand in Regulus’s curls, Harry said to the Black sisters, who had all risen to their feet and curtseyed, “We’ll hang out for a bit while my dad has a, er, chat with your mum.”

Bellatrix was aglow with an avidity bordering on starvation: lupine, unhinged. “Can I watch?”

Narcissa tugged sharply at the sash around her sister’s dress. “Don’t be uncouth, Bella. Let our lord… correct… our mother as he so wishes; we have no say in his methods, nor should we.” She bowed to Voldemort, and then to Harry. “Thank you for your help, our lord and heir, from the bottom of our hearts.”

“Please,” Harry gritted out tensely, plainly still uncomfortable with editing Walburga, “don’t mention it. And I mean that. Don’t.” He gestured at Walburga, undoing the spell he had inadvertently cast on her. For such complex magic to be unwoven so effortlessly was in itself breathtaking, and a sign that Harry did understand, on a deep, instinctual level, exactly what he had done, even if it was not an intellectual or rational understanding.

Voldemort couldn’t wait to get Harry home and grill him, taking detailed notes in preparation of replicating the spell himself.

As they all watched, Walburga melted back into life and into sentience, seemingly without having any awareness of having been a statue for the preceding twenty minutes.

Walburga promptly scowled at Regulus’s attempt to wind his way around Harry’s legs. “Don’t bother our lord’s heir,” she snapped at him, to which Harry responded by lifting Regulus into his arms and speaking right over her.

“I’m not bothered.” Harry smiled even wider. “Why, wouldn’t Regulus be my family, and you, too, Mrs Black, if I…?” Harry glanced shyly at Bellatrix in a masterful, downright Slytherin insinuation, akin to his act back in the drawing room. “Um. I assure you, I would love spending more time with,” another glance at Bellatrix, quickly redirected to Regulus, as though Regulus was merely a front for his pretence of decency, “your children.”

Walburga’s typical pallor darkened with a blotchy, gloating flush. Her hard, beady eyes glittered with greed—greed at the thought of being related to Voldemort, of the Blacks having more social prestige than any other Pureblood family in Europe. “Honoured Heir Gaunt,” she simpered, “I am overjoyed to hear that you are enjoying our… hospitality.” She smirked approvingly—and revoltingly—at Bellatrix’s cleavage. Then she barked at Kreacher, “Leave the boys here and set our best drinks out for our guests in the purple parlour downstairs.”

Voldemort decided to intervene; Walburga would not rest until Harry was alone with her daughters, which meant Voldemort had to invent a believable reason for not imprisoning her sons in this jail of a nursery. “Come, now, Walburga. Surely your boys,” who had been under house arrest since birth, “would be happy to have an outing? Perhaps a visit with…” Who could be trusted to treat them with genuine care and affection? “…the Malfoys. You have been building a closer relationship with them for years, yes?” Meaning that Voldemort was entirely cognisant of the fact that Walburga had been negotiating an engagement between the young Lucius Malfoy and her own daughter, Narcissa, who was only a year ahead of Lucius at Hogwarts and was thus a suitable match. “It would be a demonstration of your faith in them, to entrust your heirs to them for an afternoon; it would indicate that they can entrust their own heir to you.” Moreover, it would be an opportunity for Sirius and Regulus to explore the world beyond their cage… a suggestion Harry would hopefully like. Voldemort pictured Harry thanking him for it later, mouth soft with fondness and voice warm with praise.

Walburga’s calculating eyes narrowed, and she bobbed her head subserviently. “As you say, my lord. Your wisdom far outstrips mine.” Her tone hardening, she commanded Kreacher: “Hurry. Escort my sons to Mr and Mrs Malfoy via Floo, after checking that they are at home... though Mr Malfoy always tends to be. Andromeda, I entrust you with guiding our lord and his heir to our grandest parlour—”

Voldemort coughed delicately. “Perhaps you and I ought to pay a visit to your extraordinary library, Walburga? Orion informed me, once, that your collection had the rarest, most valuable texts on Necromancy.”

Walburga, like any canny Pureblood wife, instantly picked up on the subtext: that Voldemort aimed to survey the texts for any that he could ask for as dowry should his son decide to wed one of her daughters. That gloating expression ripened on Walburga’s features like a rotting fruit, obsequious and abhorrent. “Why, of course, my lord! Please, follow me to the library. Andromeda, Bellatrix… you will ensure that our lord’s heir is kept entertained in the purple parlour.”

The salacious slyness of Walburga’s instruction implied that this ‘entertainment’ would include carnal activities if the heir proved to be interested, and likely copious amounts of alcohol to facilitate those activities, given that Voldemort was well aware from his prior visits to this nightmare of a house that the purple parlour was the only parlour that had stairs leading directly down to the cellar.



Voldemort had to refrain from murdering Walburga right then and there. ‘Editing’ be damned. If only Harry didn’t have a bizarre compulsion to redeem everyone… though perhaps Voldemort shouldn’t complain, given that he himself had benefited from this compulsion.

Harry’s grip on Regulus tightened—not wanting the brothers to leave, yet—but he would have to play along in order to convince Walburga to go downstairs with his father. Harry knelt on the carpet again and embraced both Sirius and Regulus tightly, burying his face in their hair, rocking back and forth with them for a minute before releasing them. He looked both sorrowful and sweet, and so lovely that Voldemort yearned to kiss that sorrow away from his brow, to taste that sweetness for himself.

“I’ll visit you again soon, I promise,” Harry said. “At least a couple more times before I—” travel back to the future, Harry didn’t say, though his voice thickened with the prospect of that impending loss. To Sirius, he murmured: “Always remember, Sirius. You’re a good boy. A good brother. A good person. And nothing anybody says will ever change that.” There was something tremulous at the corner of Harry’s lips, something wistful and pained, but then he hugged Sirius again and rose to stand upright.

Walburga, stumped by the intensity of Harry’s words and not realising their purpose, could only peer uncomprehendingly at him. But mayhap it was to Harry’s advantage if she thought him a sentimental milksop like Orion: another man that could be browbeaten, gaslit and broken by a Black woman, subjugated like Walburga had subjugated her own husband.

After bending to plant farewell kisses on the boys’ foreheads, Harry handed them over to Kreacher, who received them with watery, grateful eyes and spirited them away to what must be the mansion’s Floo chamber.

“And now, Walburga…” Voldemort turned the full force of his blazing charm on the unsuspecting woman, who goggled at Voldemort as though he were the sun itself. “Let us give my heir and your daughters some space to get acquainted, shall we?”

She did not require more convincing.

***

Two hours hence, in the cavernous, musty library of the Blacks, Voldemort was enduring his own version of hell.

Walburga’s mind was a cesspool. It literally stank; immersing himself in it for even a second was vomit-inducing, his mental senses shrinking away from her every coiling, repugnantly slimy thought. Many of those thoughts led in directions that he outright refused to follow, for at their ends, he could detect whiffs of such spiritual ugliness that there could only be something intolerable in there—something that would force him to kill her after all, and he had promised Harry not to kill her.

He had promised. Damn it all.

Mayhap Harry’s inner purity had ruined Voldemort for the minds of others, but Walburga’s mind was… a piece of work, even compared to Mulciber’s. And while Voldemort himself often entertained violent impulses, they weren’t anywhere as putrid or predatory as Walburga’s, for whom the destruction and debasement of children were as mundane as daily chores.

Not that Walburga had many of those. Kreacher held the household and the family together in ways she did not, and, what’s more, was routinely beaten for it… for being able to do what Walburga herself could not. The brief flashes of memory that Voldemort caught of Walburga’s obscene glee in stabbing, burning, mutilating and whipping the elderly house-elf—often in the presence of the very children he cared for—were enough to repel Voldemort from seeking out more examples of her barbarity.

Unweaving the threads that wove together Walburga’s insane, depraved worldview was already tedious, but when he intuited that, in the name of training her daughters how to be traditional Pureblood wives, she had abused them more grievously than the girls had told him—had abused them, perhaps, even sexually—he had to make an executive decision.

Firstly: he would not probe further into those diseased, festering areas of her mind, not only because they were nauseatingly vile and the psychological equivalent of touching a gangrenous patient’s decaying, greenish-black, necrotic limb, but because he knew beyond the shadow of a doubt that he would murder her on the spot. Secondly: He would have to do even more ‘rewiring’—as Harry had euphemistically called it just before returning to the upstairs nursery—than Voldemort had initially estimated. Leaving Walburga free to commit more of her diabolical abuses was unthinkable.

And while Voldemort had promised Harry to alter Walburga’s psyche to reduce the likelihood of her abusing her children, Harry had, in his trusting naïveté, not thought to place an upper limit on just how far Voldemort’s… modifications… could go.

So Voldemort made it thus: that Walburga would suffer immeasurable torment while urinating and defecating, even more unbearable than the torment she’d inflicted on her children; that any abusive thought that made it out of her mouth would result in her tongue being gradually, excruciatingly liquified before an even more harrowing regeneration; that any intention to harm others through her hands, her magic or her wand would result in a temporary lobotomy that would render her insensate, twitching and drooling and soiling herself until she learned not to have those thoughts; and crucially, that none of these punishments would activate in front of Harry, so that he would never discover the severity of Voldemort’s retribution on behalf of the Black children.

All Harry would ever see would be a cowed, wordless, curiously blank Walburga… but he would have no notion of what had reduced her to that state. So ingrained was Walburga’s evil that only such extreme measures could save her children and her husband from her. The tragedy, for Harry, was that Walburga could not be ‘saved’; Walburga could not be ‘redeemed’. She was beyond redemption. Only punishment remained.

Voldemort would spare Harry the misery of that tragedy. His Harry, his innocent Harry, would never have to know.

For Voldemort was aware that, enraged as Harry was at Walburga’s behaviour, Harry wasn’t vengeful enough, spiteful enough or imaginative enough to give her the treatment she truly deserved—to give her family the justice they truly deserved. Only Voldemort could guarantee that the abuses Walburga had enacted on her children would be reflected back onto her tenfold.

A third hour passed. Longer than Voldemort had expected.

The more time he spent in Walburga’s mind, the more he was tempted to say to Harry: You should have let me kill her, after all. You should have.

Still, perhaps this was worse than death. Enslaved in her own household, reduced to less than a house-elf and in constant, wracking agony simply from performing natural bodily functions or from touching or speaking to others out of turn, Walburga would have no choice but to shut down completely, to be virtually dead whilst trapped in the shell of her body—no will, just a desperation to avoid more pain. She would not drink for fear of urinating; she would not eat for fear of defecating; she would not speak for fear of her tongue burning up and shrivelling as if under a spill of acid, only for it to torturously regrow again, a lump of raw, excoriated muscle in her mouth, bleeding and twisting. Yet her magic would not permit her to die except from old age, and Voldemort’s curse would prevent her from killing herself. A life sentence.

Oh, Voldemort would make it worse for her to live than to die. That was his gift to her children. He hoped they appreciated it.

Once he was finished—and feeling stained and dirtied by the experience—Voldemort sent Walburga to sleep on the library couch, so that the effects of his curse could take hold without being interrupted by the active cognition of a waking brain. Even asleep, she looked pinched and repulsive, malice etching lines onto her face that magic would ordinarily have erased from a middle-aged witch, had said malice not been so immense as to dwarf even her own magic’s natural tendency to heal her and keep her youthful.

Hateful shrew.

Voldemort had performed more arduous and draining acts of magic than this—such as, for example, creating his Horcruxes—but now, he was tired in a manner he had never been before. If this was what his minions’ minds were like, he’d have to screen them more carefully before granting them the honour of joining his cause. He did not intend to do this over and over again for a series of deranged Pureblood families. Merlin, no. Dealing with his own had been taxing enough. At least Morgana had character…

As he departed from the library and crossed the house in the direction of the purple parlour—he had visited the Blacks previously and was acquainted with its location—he came upon the central staircase.

At which point he realised that nobody would be in the parlour, anyway.

Because everybody was scattered over the staircase itself.

There Bellatrix was near the base of the stairs, draped over the bannister like a twine of ivy: elegant, lacy and poisonous. A tipsy flush reddened her cheeks. Sprawling beneath her on the final stair, just as inebriated, was Harry, leaning against the polished wooden balusters and giggling at some nonsensical joke of hers. He was close enough for Bellatrix to trail her fingertips along his throat—along his collar, the collar Voldemort had put on him, had buttoned for him.

Unacceptable.

Hadn’t Voldemort instructed Harry to not permit that harlot to touch him? Did he disobey Voldemort’s commands so heedlessly? Would he more readily accept the lascivious flirtations of a tawdry, buxom teenager? Had he forgotten, so easily, to whom he belonged?

The situation was made barely palatable by the discouraging and dampening scrutiny of the two other Black sisters. Andromeda and Narcissa must not have partaken of any alcohol at all, what with Narcissa’s steely gaze keeping a hawk-like watch on the proceedings and Andromeda frequently interrupting Bellatrix’s attempts at seduction by physically interceding under the pretence of rescuing a tipping glass or removing an empty bottle, or verbally interceding by making frequent, disparaging comments about Bellatrix’s multiple lovers, as though to imply to Harry that he wasn’t too special, and that Bellatrix’s fawning over him wasn’t too special, either.

That Bellatrix would dare to touch Harry was affront enough—but that she would touch him as just another notch on her proverbial broomstick, as just another disposable, was an offence against the very order of the universe. Harry was irreplaceable. Anybody who saw him as less than that was undeserving of him.

Keeping the frown off his brow by main force, Voldemort instead smiled benevolently as he arrived at the staircase. All the sisters arose in greeting, though Bella was embarrassingly unsteady. Only Harry did not arise.

“Oh, hi!” Harry beamed up at him, tilting precariously to the left as he lifted his elaborately engraved crystal chalice, which was half-full of a sapphire-blue liquid that glittered like a jewel and gave off a silvery, aromatic mist. “You’ve got to try this medieval mermaid wine! It’s the best. You can drink it underwater! Even hangover potions won’t work on it! No sobering spells, either!” Harry praised it as if he’d personally overseen the wine’s brewing, only to list forward towards Voldemort and whisper loudly, “Once you’re drunk on it, you’re drunk good and proper.”

There was nothing ‘good’ or ‘proper’ about what Bellatrix’s fingers were doing at that very instant; thankfully, Narcissa took one look at Voldemort’s falsely pleasant mien and stepped in to pluck her sister’s hand from the Gaunt heir in the begrudging, gingerly manner of one plucking a filthy handkerchief off the floor. Her distaste for her sister’s shenanigans was mollifying. Had it not been, Bellatrix’s fingers would have been forfeit.

“Ladies,” Voldemort greeted, though the title certainly did not fit Bellatrix. He wrapped an arm around Harry’s shoulders and helped him to his feet, where Harry teetered, leaning against him. “It is time for us to depart. Your mother is… resting… in the library, and I would ask you to leave her undisturbed until she awakens tomorrow morning. Your patience will be rewarded by a mother much more—” how to phrase this tactfully? “—attentive and accommodating to your needs.”

Harry’s effervescence dulled at this revelation, though his features eased with a profound relief, as well. “Thanks for that. I hafta… gotta talk to Kreacher… I…” He shrugged off Voldemort’s arm absently—another affront, since he hadn’t shrugged off Bellatrix—before tottering towards the nearby kitchen in pursuit of the errant house-elf, who had disappeared altogether after depositing Sirius and Regulus at the Malfoys’. “Kreacher…? I have some, um… instructions… ideas… Hello?”

As Harry wandered off in search of the house-elf, the Black sisters greeted him in return.

“My lord.” Narcissa curtseyed. “We are forever in your debt.”

“Nonsense.” Though Narcissa was right; Voldemort would claim a repayment eventually, such as, for example, access to their vault in search of the device that had brought Harry to him… and might take Harry away. “You are all under my protection. It is my duty to keep you safe.”

Narcissa raised an eyebrow and withdrew to let Andromeda take the lead.

Andromeda approached, her generally unremarkable countenance sharpened and whetted as if by a knife—the knife of her hatred for her mother. “If I might ask, my lord, to what extent is our mother altered? If she attempts further… actions, like those we described, what are we do to in response?”

Voldemort folded his arms before him as he’d seen the monks at Aldgate Priory do during a Christmas outing from Wool’s Orphanage: meditative, serene. Out of the corner of his eye, he spotted Harry now chatting away with a newly materialised Kreacher just past the open kitchen door, putting Harry firmly beyond hearing range. “All you have to say is ‘stop’, and she will stop. Completely. She will not speak or move until you permit her to do so again.”

Narcissa’s eyes widened. Bellatrix’s eyes gleamed.

As for Andromeda, she asked carefully: “My lord, are you saying that we can put our sainted mother into an induced coma whenever we want to?”

“Will she feel pain when she’s like that?” Bellatrix enquired eagerly, evidently thinking of torturing her mother in that vulnerable state—and how telling it was that neither of her sisters chastised her for the savagery of the question.

Hm. A reassuring reminder that Walburga really was that bad. Voldemort had acted justly here today, even if Harry, with his many scruples, might disagree. “That is for you to find out,” Voldemort offered generously, aware that he might be twinkling like Dumbledore, of all people.

Bellatrix responded with a dazzling, childlike smile.

Harry wouldn’t have been comfortable with Voldemort’s more severe punishments of Walburga, but given Harry’s intoxicated state and his rambling monologue to a confused yet indulgent Kreacher in the kitchen, he didn’t seem to have heard.

Good.

“I suggest that you not trouble Harry, nor indeed your father or your acquaintances, with my corrective measures in regards to Walburga. It is of vital importance, I’m sure you’ll agree, that the reputation of House Black not be further tarnished, and that their rank amongst the Death Eaters not be jeopardised. Those that infer my involvement in Walburga’s… unexpected docility might do so, but their inferences shall not be shaped by any information volunteered by any of you.”

“Of course not, my lord!” Bellatrix bowed so low that she almost fell over, her bosom all but bursting forth from its insufficient confines within her slipping gown.

Voldemort resisted the urge to curl his upper lip in contempt. Had she not been a potential recruit by virtue of her magical talent, he would not have bothered to disguise his ire… not that Narcissa appeared to be duped by his disguise, alert and watchful as she was, standing dispassionately to the side.

Sensible girl. Also a potential recruit, in her case for her discernment—sadly, a rare trait amongst his Death Eaters.

So he nodded in approval, retrieved his drunken son from the kitchen where he had somehow managed to reduce Kreacher to blubbering gratitude, and Apparated both Harry and himself home from the front porch.

At last. At last he would be alone with Harry, away from the prying eyes and greedy fingers of lustful, overreaching brats like Bellatrix.

With him, finally, Harry would be safe.

Chapter 22


Chapter Summary

So far, this story has posed the very serious question: “How horny can one man get without realising it?” And Voldemort’s answer to that question has just been: “Yes.”

That changes today. Oh, does it change. The man finally realises he’s horny, folks. The greatest epiphany of the century. Give this genius the Nobel Prize.

Today’s headline in the Daily Prophet should totally be: SHY MAIDEN DARK LORD DISCOVERS HE HAS A LIBIDO. OPERATION SIMPTASTIC: LAUNCH! Heck, he simps so (heh) hard that he actually grows a conscience. You okay there, Voldemort?

Yeah, so this chapter is basically just Voldemort having one long, continuous heart attack. I’d apologise, but I know you’ll enjoy it… as will I. (As will Voldemort once he lets himself relax, sheesh.)

Please note that Voldemort is demisexual in this story and is not used to desiring anyone, so to him, this desire initially seems to be very against his nature and against how he is used to interacting with people… particularly Harry, whom he adores so much that the thought of objectifying him with lust just feels kind of wrong. This dude, y’all. He’s such a special snowflake. A scary, murderous snowflake, but still.

Immediately upon Apparating himself and Harry into their parlour, Voldemort was accosted by Flopsy.

She was holding a carving knife more than three-quarters her size. An unwashed carving knife. Strands of meat still clung to it, gelatinous and pink.

“Master Lord Voldemort,” she greeted with a smile that was more of a grimace, though it grew considerably fonder when directed at Harry. “Master Harry.” She approached him with an expression of swiftly growing concern.

Harry lifted a woozy hand, grinning lopsidedly. “’Sup, Flo?”

Flopsy’s face darkened like a tiny thundercloud. She scowled accusingly at Voldemort. “You drugged him.”

“I. What? No.” Then Voldemort realised how absurd it was to be defending himself to a miniature enslaved creature whose wrinkly pate barely reached his knee. “Your darling boy merely got himself inebriated with a teenage girl who should have known better. As should he.”

Harry nodded amiably. “He’s right, she gave me these mermaids. On bottles. Mermaid bottles. Very nice. Very… blue.” Harry gesticulated as if he could physically describe a shade of blue, nearly smacking Voldemort’s nose in the process.

“Master Lord Voldemort let Master Harry get drunk on mermaid wine?” Flopsy thunked the tip of the blade into the wooden floorboards below, leaving a dent. “This is not what responsible parents be doing.”

Why on earth was Voldemort letting this pustulant little critter judge his parenting? “I hardly let him—”

“He was busy with something suh—hic—super important, don’t be mad, Flopsy.” Harry reached down to pat Flopsy’s shoulder cheerfully, almost missing it. “I’m safe! The big bad wolf didn’t even take a bite, damn him.” Harry half-snickered, half-hiccuped. “I mean, I was wearing red and everything…”

Voldemort frowned. Harry wasn’t wearing red. What was he on about? And what wolf?

Clearly, his son was in dire need of a sobering potion, but given that those didn’t work on mermaid wine, he’d have to get Harry upstairs, bathed and in his bed to sleep off the effects.

When he moved to guide Harry up the stairs, however, Flopsy intervened, putting her small frame between Voldemort and the staircase. “Master Harry is being drunk,” she stated flatly. “Flopsy is being the one to put him to bed. Master Lord Voldemort is welcome to relax in the parlour, where I will be bringing him his supper.”

“I don’t require any supper. You’re dismissed.”

Flopsy didn’t budge. “It is not a good idea for Master Lord Voldemort to be touching Master Harry when he is being drunk.” She lifted her knife and inspected its glinting edge meditatively. “Master Lord Voldemort must be remembering my warning, surely.”

What, the elf’s ludicrous threat of poisoning him? “I could reduce you to a pinch of ash with a thought, you realise.”

“You could certainly be trying.”

They glared at each other.

Harry would probably never smile at Voldemort again if he harmed Flopsy.

Flopsy’s beady eyes shone with victory as a whole minute ticked by without Voldemort casting so much as a jelly-legs jinx.

Damned vermin. “I commanded you to leave. Have you forgotten that ancient ancestral magic compels you to obey me?”

“You is not being the only master of your ancestry in this house. Master Harry is not telling me to go. Maybe, me is thinking, Master Harry does not want me to leave. Maybe, Master Harry is not feeling safe.” A pause. “With you.”

The nerve—

Harry, who had been witnessing Voldemort’s and Flopsy’s exchange raptly, as if it were the highest form of entertainment, snorted. “Flopsy. You can go rest. Honestly. You shouldn’t be up this late doing…” He regarded her carving knife with bemusement. “…meal prep, or whatever.”

“That is still not being a command.” Flopsy raised her chin. “It is being a suggestion.”

“You—” Voldemort stepped forward, his patience evaporating.

“Flopsy, you should go,” Harry encouraged her, slurred but gentle. “He would rather cut off his own hands than hurt me. I don’t feel even a bit unsafe.”

“But you ‘is wearing red, and everything’,” Flopsy pointed out, echoing Harry’s gibberish back at him. “And the wolf is having lots of teeth.”

Wait. Was Voldemort supposed to be the wolf? Why?

“Flopsy.” Harry sighed. “Go.”

With a last mutter about stupid human children with no sense of self-preservation, Flopsy directed a final, warning glower at Voldemort before vanishing with a pop.

“Why does she believe so strenuously that I intend to hurt you?”

“Eh,” said Harry vaguely, wobbling towards the stairway, only for Voldemort to seize him by the elbow before he tripped. “She’s just overprotective of the…” Harry stumbled and cast a betrayed glance at his uncooperative feet. “…um, the heir of the bloodline she serves.”

That was patently untrue. Flopsy’s loyalty was fiercely, fanatically personal to Harry, and Harry knew it. Why was he pretending otherwise? To shield Flopsy from Voldemort’s wrath, perhaps?

“I would not injure her,” Voldemort clarified, affronted. “Not when you care for her.”

“I know.” And Harry’s voice was damnably gentle again; it galled Voldemort that he, the Dark Lord, and a house-elf were apparently equal in Harry’s assessment, to be subjected to the same benevolent mollification.

Well, the interfering old nanny was gone. Voldemort wound his arm around Harry’s waist with a ‘so there’ attitude that embarrassed even him, before he shrugged the incident off and focused on guiding Harry up the stairs. He could just Apparate Harry there, but then, he would have to let go of Harry sooner.

***

Harry, as Voldemort had guessed, insisted on bathing before bed; it must be a habit he had gained at Hogwarts, much like Tom Riddle had done, although Voldemort had since developed a liking for bathing earlier.

Still, Voldemort hesitated. Water tended to amplify mermaid wine’s effects, but it got rid of them faster, too, which meant that Harry wouldn’t be beleaguered by them tomorrow. Harry would no doubt prefer having amplified symptoms now, for the short interval before he slept, than having to suffer through another day of alcohol-induced infirmity. Humans metabolised mermaid wine more inefficiently than mermaids themselves did; Flopsy was not incorrect in her assessment of Harry’s stupidity in drinking that wine… and Voldemort’s stupidity in letting Harry drink it.

Nonetheless, Voldemort had spelled the bathtub in Harry’s washroom to drain instantly at the slightest sign of its inhabitant drowning. Even if Harry was overcome by the wine and sank beneath the water, he would be unable to drown, as the water would then disappear.

Thus reassured, Voldemort permitted Harry to waddle into the bathroom, shrugging his clothes off haphazardly as he went.

Voldemort averted his hungry eyes out of some remnant of decency that he had so far been unaware of possessing, but Harry didn’t give him a choice about remaining decent. Voldemort’s well-intentioned, determined study of the arched, mint-green ceiling above them was interrupted by an armful of slippery fabric being thrown at him.

“Mmph!” Voldemort caught Harry’s shirt a moment too late, after the black silk had landed on his face. The cloth was tantalisingly pine-scented, with a salty trace of young, clean sweat and the musky sea-tinge of mermaid wine. Best of all, beneath all those fragrances was the enchanting, rainforest-and-lightning essence of Harry’s magic, static electricity tingling in the wake of that smooth silk. Voldemort inhaled deeply before regretfully discarding Harry’s shirt in the laundry basket; it wouldn’t do for Harry to turn around and discover Voldemort sniffing Harry’s clothing like a common underwear thief.

Harry’s actual underwear did not hit Voldemort, sparing him what would have been an existential crisis. Instead, Harry’s trousers and underwear were kicked off his pretty ankles to puddle next to the tub, and Voldemort stared at them, overwhelmed, paralysed between the equally powerful urges to look up and to preserve Harry’s virtue.

It occurred to him that Flopsy had rightly judged Voldemort incompetent at servile duties that required visual intimacy. Harry’s nudity would not have rendered her incapable of movement, speech… or even thought. The realisation was humiliating.

Voldemort stood there, frozen, as a distinctly floral steam rose around him, thickening into a palpable mist. Water sloshed quietly in the background. Then a sigh so blissful escaped Harry that it veritably tore Voldemort’s gaze from the ceiling—where it had, once again, affixed itself in self-defence—to where Harry was now thankfully submerged in bubbly, soapy water, save for his pale shoulders and his flushed, beautiful face. Translucent droplets slid down his neck to pool and glimmer in his collarbones. His mouth parted as if in a wordless satiation, his lips made softer and redder by the heat.

To make matters worse, Harry had tipped his head back against the ceramic tub’s rounded edge, his eyes closed, his throat bared.

Utterly unguarded.

Age had left its marks on the pitted wood, minute dents and pockmarks under the gleaming polish—imperfections that Voldemort had not noticed until today. Then again, he had never sat so long at this table, contemplating the patterns in its wooden grain in lieu of dwelling on the events of last night.

This was just another unremarkable morning. That was what Voldemort had to believe. From years of observing his Hogwarts schoolmates conclude that they did not in fact want the people they’d pursued after they’d fornicated with them, Voldemort had gathered that attraction was a strange and unpredictable force, and that attraction itself did not automatically translate into a desire or an ability to bed that person. There had been that Hufflepuff prefect, Lorna Meadowes, who had once tearfully confided to a befuddled Abraxas that she did want him, just not like that…

Whatever Harry had intended by his disclosure, or by peeking shyly at Voldemort through his eyelashes as he was now doing, that was—that was—

So he distracted himself with magical theory until his heartbeat evened out, until his ears ceased ringing like bells.

Harry had interacted with two of his Horcruxes. Two. The Voldemort of the future had either trusted Harry enough to give Harry unfettered access to his Hocruxes—which, given Harry’s fundamental goodness, might even be believable—or Harry had been up to no good.

Voldemort decided not to ask. Regardless of Harry’s motives, it was incredible that Harry had conversed with Voldemort’s Horcruxes—that Horcruxes weren’t merely inert soul fragments but were alive, opinionated and sentient.

He would have to liberate the Horcruxes he had already created, and soon. The idea of pieces of himself being imprisoned forever, after all his efforts to escape imprisonment in Wool’s and in bomb bunkers, horrified him, much as the notion of torturing his own progeny had horrified him when he’d stumbled upon Harry.

Harry, meanwhile, chuckled. He had begun drinking his tea, his posture relaxed as it hadn’t been before, though his cheeks were still pink. “Have I broken you again, Tom? My apologies.”

Voldemort sent him a weak glare.

Harry set his teacup aside and buttered his toast. He cleared his throat again and said, “I hope I didn’t pressure you with my… yeah. Confession, or whatever. I mean, it’s kinda silly to describe you of all people as feeling pressured, but I guess you could be if it’s by someone you love.” Then Harry realised what he’d just said and shot up in his chair, ramrod-straight, dropping his toast as if it burned. “Um! Not that you—”

“I do.” Voldemort’s statement was ironclad, his voice deep and grave. Harry seemed to melt where he sat, growing pinker and sliding down in his seat as Voldemort spoke. “Of course I do, Harry. Could my love be a question at this point?”

Harry covered his face with his hands. “Oh, god,” he said, sounding hushed and amazed and somewhat panicked.

“Did I break you, Harry?” Voldemort retorted teasingly, only for Harry to stop hiding behind his hands and glare at him in return.

Then an unholy mischief lit Harry’s green, green eyes, and Voldemort experienced a flash of absolute, unfettered, existential dread when Harry pulled the platter of tangerines closer.

Voldemort should have seen this coming. He should have known that Harry would never surrender without a fight. But alas, he could only intuit what Harry was going to do seconds before Harry did it: picking up a slice of tangerine and licking it with a tongue that had no right to be so clever, so mobile.

Voldemort seized up. All his bodily functions ground to a halt, though he supposed he must be breathing to still be living.

The tangerines were a vivid orange in the sunshine flooding into the kitchen, and gave off a fresh, citrusy scent—a scent that would undoubtedly taste delectable in Harry’s mouth. Tart. Sweet. Irresistible.

Immobile as if under a Petrificus Totalus, Voldemort could only sit there and gape, helpless, as Harry watched him through half-lidded, hazy eyes and ate the tangerines slice after slice. It was a masterful seduction precisely because it wasn’t confident: Harry’s fingers were trembling all the while, and he was blushing so fiercely that even his ears were red. Red and adorable and biteable. Voldemort pictured biting the dainty tips of those ears as gently as Harry was biting his gods-be-damned tangerines.

Inevitably, juice meandered down Harry’s slender wrist, a glittering trail that Harry chased with his tongue. All the way down to his inner elbow, a path shining with spit.

And that primitive part of Voldemort’s, so long ignored, twitched and thickened against Voldemort’s thigh. His chair’s wooden armrests creaked under his grip.

Harry panted. His flush spread down his neck and beyond his clavicles, and when he finished his final slice, he slowly ran his juice-slick fingertips down his throat until they reached his pyjama top’s first button.

“Remember the party?” Harry said hoarsely. “This is my revenge.”

The armrests of Voldemort’s chair shattered.

Harry jumped, eyes wide. “Wha—”

Voldemort, whose brain seemed to have vacated his body, peered clinically down at the splinters embedded in his palms.

Harry got up hastily. “Oh, fuck, I’m—I’m so sorry, I—”

“Harry,” Voldemort heard himself say as if from far away, from another dimension, even, “I will write to Headmaster Dippet requesting a school visit. We must recover Ravenclaw’s Diadem so that I may reabsorb the Horcrux within it.”

“I…” If Harry was suffering whiplash from the change in topic, he didn’t show it. “Uh, sure?”

Voldemort stood. His magic knitted the tiny, bloody gashes on his palms closed. The splinters fell out as his skin healed, and he brushed them off against his sleep trousers—trousers that Harry immediately fixated on, no doubt taking note of the evidence of Voldemort’s arousal, heavy and full, dangerously tight against the confines of inadequate stitching. Despite being the direct cause of Voldemort’s state, Harry had the gall to look shocked and, yes, even afraid.

Served him right. Voldemort’s size had often intimidated his former partners, had brought some of them pain and reduced others to tears. Let Harry be put off by it, then. Let Harry be nervous at Voldemort’s glowing, inhuman gaze, all hellfire and brimstone.

Harry flinched a little as Voldemort approached him, as Voldemort towered over him and studied him as one might an object within one’s possession that could be touched, could be used, could be broken whenever one wanted.

Harry shivered.

He was less taunting now, less brazen—humbled by the consequences of his actions, though there was still a heat in his eyes that had not been extinguished—that had, against the odds, flared even hotter.

A pity. The challenge of self-control fell to Voldemort, as always.

Wood shards and sawdust from his chair crunched under his feet as he walked past Harry. The chair’s armrests reassembled themselves behind him.

“Whoa,” Harry said tremulously, sinking back onto his own chair with shaky legs. “Wow.”

Voldemort ignored him. He exited the kitchen and climbed back up the stairs, towards the attic that had been converted into an Owlery.

Dippet had better heed his request, or else.
